L930: ## 2.2 Sponsor's Conclusions
L931: - The pharmacokinetics of lixisenatide following subcutaneous injection was best described by a one-compartment model, with a first order absorption (k_a), with IIV and IOV on apparent clearance (CL/F), apparent volume of distribution (V/F) and mean absorption time (MAT=1/k_a), and with a combined additive and proportional residual error (in the log domain).
L932: - Among the covariates tested, *CLCR* was the most important covariate for explaining the IIV of total lixisenatide CL/F; dose (tested in the range of 5 µg QD to 30 µg BID) was the most important covariate for explaining the IIV of the MAT.
- Body weight had a major effect on the IIV of V/F. No isolated effect of gender, race, or age was identified.
- The remaining IIV (not explained by covariate effects) was 40.3%, 30.3% and 30.0% on CL/F, V/F and MAT, respectively (in log domain). The IOV (44.9% to 59.3%) was higher than the IIV, suggesting a limited potential for dose optimization.
L933: ## **Reviewer's comments on Sponsor's Population PK Analysis:**
L934: - *Sponsor's population PK analysis is generally adequate and acceptable for the purpose of labeling recommendations.*
- *During base model development sponsor excluded some observations if log-transformed observed concentrations exceeded the IPRED by more than 2. Sponsor did not provide any adequate rationale for this exclusion of observation. The excluded observations were only about 3% of the total samples used in the analysis. Thus, it is assumed that these exclusions have very little impact on the overall analysis.*
- *Based on population PK analysis, creatinine clearance was identified as significant covariate for clearance and body weight was identified as significant covariate affecting volume of distribution of lixisenatide. Although creatinine clearance is identified as a covariate, the inclusion of creatinine clearance did not reduce the inter-individual variability on clearance. The conclusions regarding dose adjustment based on renal function will be derived based on dedicated phase 1 trial.*
- *Sponsor's conclusion that no dose adjustment based on age, gender, body weight and race is acceptable. See reviewer's independent analysis in section 3.3.*
- *Sponsor conducted dedicated Phase 1 trials to see the impact of age and race on the pharmacokinetics of lixisenatide. Population PK analysis is supportive to these dedicated Phase 1 trials.*
L935: ## 3 Results of Reviewer's Analysis
L936: ## 3.1 Objectives
L937: The primary objective was to confirm sponsor's claim that there are no clinically meaningful effects of gender, race, body weight, and creatinine clearance on lixisenatide pharmacokinetics.

L945: ## 3.3 Results
L946: There is an increase in lixisenatide clearance with increasing creatinine clearance (Figures 12 A and B). The mean clearance in subjects with severe, moderate, and mild renal impairment is 65%, 40%, and 22% lower, respectively, than that of normal renal function subjects. After inclusion of creatinine clearance in sponsor's final model, no systematic trend between inter-individual variability on clearance and creatinine clearance is observed (Figure 12C).
L947: There is a trend for increasing lixisenatide clearance with increase in body weight (Figure 13A). However, body weight was found to be highly correlated to cCrCL and thus after inclusion of creatinine clearance in the final model no systematic trend between inter-individual variability on clearance and body weight as observed in Figure 13B.
L948: There is also a trend for decrease in clearance with increasing age (Figure 14A and 14B). Age was found to be highly correlated to cCrCL and thus after inclusion of creatinine clearance in the final model resulted in no systematic trend between inter-individual variability on clearance and age (Figure 14C).
L949: Gender and race do not significantly affect the clearance of lixisenatide. Figure 15 and Figure 16 show that the inter-individual variability in clearance cannot be explained by gender and race respectively.
L950: Figure 12: A) Scatter plot of clearance vs. creatinine clearance, B) Boxplot of clearance vs. creatinine clearance and C) Inter-individual variability on clearance vs. creatinine clearance
L951: Figure 13: A) Scatter plot for clearance vs. weight B) Inter-individual variability on clearance vs. weight
L952: Figure 14: A) Scatter plot for clearance vs. age B) Boxplot of clearance vs. age and C) Inter-individual variability on clearance vs. age
L953: Figure 15: A) Clearance and B) Inter-individual variability on clearance vs. gender

## Thorough QT Study Review
L958: IND	62724

L959: Note: Any text in the review with a light background or quoted should be inferred as copied from the sponsor's document.
L960: ## 1 SUMMARY
L961: ## 1.1 OVERALL SUMMARY OF FINDINGS
L962: The largest upper bounds of the 2-sided 90% CI for the mean difference between lixisenatide (20 µg q.d. and 30 µg b.i.d.) and placebo were below 10 ms, the threshold for regulatory concern as described in ICH E14 guidelines. The largest lower bound of the two-sided 90% CI for the \Delta\Delta QTcF for moxifloxacin was higher than 5 ms, and the moxifloxacin profile over time is demonstrated in Figure 3, indicating that assay sensitivity was established.
L963: In this randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, multiple dose, placebo-controlled parallel study, 91 healthy subjects received lixisenatide 20 µg once daily, lixisenatide 30 µg twice daily, placebo, and a single oral dose of moxifloxacin 400 mg. Overall summary of findings is presented in Table 1.
L964: Table 1: The Point Estimates and the 90% CIs Corresponding to the Largest Upper Bounds for AVE0010 (20 µg and 30 µg) and the Largest Lower Bound for Moxifloxacin (FDA Analysis)
L965: Treatment	Time (hour)	ΔΔQTcF (ms)	90% CI (ms)
Lixisenatide 20 µg q.d.	3	4.6	(1.8, 7.5)
Lixisenatide 30 µg b.i.d.	3	5.5	(2.6, 8.3)
Moxifloxacin 400 mg*	3	12.0	(8.5, 15.4)
L966: * Multiple endpoint adjustment of 3 time points was applied.
L967: The selection of 20-µg q.d. dose as the therapeutic dose appears to be reasonable. The supratherapeutic dose (30 µg b.i.d.) represents the highest dose tested. The overall exposure as well as the variability of the pharmacokinetic parameters increased largely in the antibody-positive subjects. The pharmacokinetic data in this study covered a wide range of lixisenatide exposure with mean values of C_{max} up to 209 pg/mL and AUClast up to 979 pg*h/mL in antibody-negative subjects, and C_{max} up to 945 pg/mL and AUClast up to 12500 pg*h/mL in antibody-positive subjects. The mean C_{max} and AUC of lixisenatide was approximately 40% higher at 30 µg b.i.d. than at 20 µg q.d. in antibody-negative subjects or antibody-positive subjects. Significant but shallow exposure-response relationship was observed. However, there is no substantial QT prolongation in the exposure range at the therapeutic dose of 20 µg q.d. and the supratherapeutic dose of 30 µg b.i.d.
L968: Lixisenatide exposure is significantly increased in patients with severe renal impairment. However, the increase in lixisenatide exposure (e.g., C_{max}) was not reported (Highlights of Clinical Pharmacology). Whether or not supratherapeutic dose is adequate to cover the worst case high exposure clinical scenario will depend on the exposure increase in severe renal impairment.
L969: ## 2 BACKGROUND
L970: ## 2.1 PRODUCT INFORMATION
L971: Lixisenatide is a GLP-1 receptor agonist.
L972: ## 2.2 MARKET APPROVAL STATUS
L973: Lixisenatide is not approved for marketing in any country.
L974: ## 2.3 PRECLINICAL INFORMATION
L975: From IB (14 March 2012)
L976: At a concentration of 0.57 µg/mL, lixisenatide had no effect on resting membrane potential and action potential parameters in electrically stimulated rabbit Purkinje fibers (DSE2005-0098). A moderate and dose-dependent block of delayed rectified potassium currents by lixisenatide was observed in Chinese hamster ovary cells stably expressing
L977: the human ether-à-go-go related gene channel; maximum inhibition was 37.3% at the highest concentration tested (30 µg/mL) (DSE2003-1521). It was not possible to test the effect of lixisenatide up to a concentration of 100 µg/mL, as initially intended, since at any higher concentration the compound disrupted the seal between cell and electrode. The reason for this phenomenon is unknown, but might be due to a surface-active property of lixisenatide. However, the lowest concentration tested was four orders of magnitude higher than Cmax in clinical studies at 20 µg q.d., indicating a large exposure margin.
L978: In conscious rats, IV administration of lixisenatide increased mean arterial pressure by approximately 25% at 50 µg/kg, with no further increase with cumulative dosing (00-127). This effect on mean arterial pressure is described to be rat-specific for GLP-1-agonists in the literature.
L979: In anesthetized Beagle dogs, no effect on blood pressure and other cardiovascular parameters (ECG, femoral artery blood flow and resistance) was seen (DSE2004-1182). In addition, no effects significantly different from controls regarding respiratory parameters have been observed after lixisenatide doses of up to 10 µg/kg IV.
L980: In anesthetized dogs, a 30-minute infusion of lixisenatide at 10 µg/kg induced a moderate but statistically significant decrease in serum glucose concentration, reaching a maximum of 1.43 \pm 0.22 mmol/L at the end of infusion, a slight but significant increase in heart rate at mid infusion and at the end of infusion, reaching a maximum of +5 \pm 1.6 bpm at mid infusion, and a marginal but significant decrease of PQ interval duration at mid infusion related to heart rate increase and reaching a maximum of 2 \pm 0.9 ms. No effects on potassium concentration, body temperature, QRS complex duration and on QT (corrected or not) interval duration were observed (CRV0345).
L981: The combination lixisenatide followed with insulin glargine induced a significant decrease in serum glucose concentration, reaching a maximum of 3.78 \pm 0.32 mmol/L at 30 minutes after administration, a significant decrease in serum potassium concentration, reaching a maximum of 1.1 \pm 0.25 mmol/L at 15 minutes after administration, a significant increase in heart rate, reaching a maximum of +16 \pm 5.3 bpm at 45 minutes post dosing, reversible ECG morphology modifications up to 60 minutes post injection (minor ST segment elevation, T wave morphology and amplitude modifications), and an increase in QT interval duration (corrected or not), reaching a maximum of +15 \pm 4.5 ms, +26 \pm 3.5 ms and +19 \pm 3.2 ms for QT, QTcF, and QT interval corrected according to van de Water formula (QTcW) respectively, significant at 15 minutes. The effects on QTcF and QTcW remained significant from 15 to 90 and 15 to 60 min post dosing, respectively. No effect on PQ interval duration QRS complex duration occurred (CVR0345). These effects are probably linked to decreased potassium serum concentrations caused by insulin glargine.
L982: Reviewer's comments: Lixisenatide inhibits hERG currents with low affinity. The combination of lixisenatide and insulin decreases serum glucose concentration, potassium concentration, increases heart rate and QTc duration.
L983: ## 2.4 PREVIOUS CLINICAL EXPERIENCE
L984: From IB (14 March 2012)
L985: At the cut-off for this document, 960 subjects and patients have been treated in 9 completed studies, and 735 thereof have been exposed to AVE0010. Safety in T2DM has been assessed in a total of 754 patients with 573 exposed to AVE0010 (555 with repeated-dose regimen).
L986: A thorough QT/QTc study (TES6865) investigated the potential for delayed ventricular repolarization (QT interval prolongation) after multiple-doses of lixisenatide at the maintenance dose of 20 µg q.d. and at a supratherapeutic dose of 30 µg b.i.d.. At both doses of lixisenatide, there were no relevant or statistically significant mean increases in the QTcF or PR interval. There was no relevant change in heart rate in the 20 µg q.d. group, but at the supratherapeutic dose, the mean heart rate versus placebo increased by 4.73 beats per minute (bpm). A total of 91 subjects enrolled in the study were evaluated for safety. Two subjects discontinued the study due to an adverse event (1 due to urticaria in the 30 µg b.i.d. and 1 due to pruritic rash in the 20 µg q.d. regimens of AVE0010 on Day 14 and Day 22, respectively). No deaths or serious adverse events were reported during the study
L987: A central reading of ECGs was also conducted in the Phase 3 Study EFC6018 in which 239 patients were exposed to lixisenatide. At the maintenance dose of 20 µg q.d., no adverse findings on QTcF (-0.93 ms versus 2.63 ms for placebo) or heart rate (-0.6 bpm versus 1.3 bpm for placebo) were detected. In this study, 139 (55%) patients were antibody-positive. In conclusion, at the maintenance dose of 20 µg q.d., lixisenatide revealed no adverse ECG findings including in antibody-positive patients with T2DM (Study EFC6018).
L988: *Reviewer's comments: The sponsor conducted previously a TQT study TES6865. QT-IRT reviewed the study report and concluded that the study was inconclusive because assay sensitivity could not be demonstrated. No AEs of concern as per ICH E14 Guidance were reported.*
L989: *No seizures, sudden deaths or ventricular arrhythmias were reported. No clinically relevant ECG changes were reported.*

L992: ## 3 SPONSOR'S SUBMISSION
L993: ## 3.1 OVERVIEW
L994: The QT-IRT did not review the protocol prior to conducting this study. The sponsor submitted the study report TES6865 for AVE0010, including electronic datasets and waveforms to the ECG warehouse.
L995: ## 3.2 TQT STUDY
L996: ## 3.2.1 Title
L997: "Effect of repeated subcutaneous injections (20 µg once daily and 30 µg twice daily) of lixisenatide on ventricular repolarization, compared to placebo with moxifloxacin (400 mg single dose) as a positive control, in healthy subjects. A randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, 4-parallel group study."
L998: ## 3.2.2 Protocol Number
L999: TES11807
L1000: ## 3.2.3 Study Dates
L1001: 05 January 2011 -- 28 September 2011
L1002: ## 3.2.4 Objectives
L1003: ## Primary objective:
L1004: "To assess the effect of a standard therapeutic and a supra-therapeutic dose regimen of lixisenatide administered as a repeated dose (14 days dose escalation + 14 days maintenance dose of 20 µg q.d. and 30 µg b.i.d.) on QTcF interval compared to placebo.
L1005: ## Secondary objectives:
L1006: - To assess the effect of lixisenatide on heart rate (HR), QT, QTcB, and QTcN, compared to placebo;
- To assess the clinical and laboratory safety of lixisenatide;
- To determine the plasma concentration of lixisenatide at the time of ECG investigation and assess the steady state pharmacokinetics of lixisenatide following repeated doses;
- To assess anti-lixisenatide antibody development after repeated dosing."
L1007: ## 3.2.5 Study Description
L1008: ## 3.2.5.1 Design
L1009: This was a single center, randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, multiple dose, placebo-controlled study, and conducted in 4 parallel groups.
L1010: ## 3.2.5.2 Controls
L1011: The Sponsor used both placebo and positive (moxifloxacin) controls.
L1012: ## 3.2.5.3 Blinding
L1013: All treatment arms were administered blinded using a double dummy approach. Moxifloxacin tablets were over encapsulated.
L1014: ## 3.2.6 Treatment Regimen
L1015: ## 3.2.6.1 Treatment Arms
L1016: The study was conducted in a 4-parallel group design and each subject received one of the 4 treatments as presented in following table:

L1021: *Source: CSR Section 8.1 Table 1*
L1022: ## 3.2.6.2 Sponsor's Justification for Doses
L1023: A dose of 20 µg q.d. lixisenatide was shown to significantly lower HbA1c after 4 weeks of treatment. Therefore a dose of 20 µg q.d. is considered to already represent a therapeutically effective dose and may be potentially selected as the therapeutic standard dose. A dose of 30 µg b.i.d. is considered as a supratherapeutic condition and is therefore selected for the second dose arm in this TES study. The dose regimen of 30 µg b.i.d. has been tested in two clinical studies (DRI6012, TES6865) and was found to be safe.
L1024: The two maintenance doses will be preceded by two different dose titration schemes. For 20 µg q.d., the preceding doses are 10 µg q.d. in the first week and 15 µg q.d. in the second week. For 30 µg b.i.d., the preceding doses are 10 µg b.i.d. in the first week and 20 µg b.i.d. in the second week. Therefore, it is assumed that the observations made in this study will cover future therapeutic and supra-therapeutic exposures.
L1025: *Reviewer's Comments: The selection of 20-µg q.d. as the therapeutic dose appears to be reasonable. The 30 µg b.i.d represents the largest dose tested and is chosen as the supratherapeutic dose. The overall exposure as well as the variability of the pharmacokinetic parameters increased largely in the antibody-positive subjects. The pharmacokinetic data in this study covered a wide range of lixisenatide exposure with mean values of Cmax up to 209 pg/mL and AUClast up to 979 pg*h/mL in antibody-*
L1026: *negative subjects, and C_{max} up to 945 pg/mL and AUClast up to 12500 pg*h/mL in antibody-positive subjects.*
L1027: *Whether or not supratherapeutic dose is adequate will depend on the exposure increase in severe renal impairment. Lixisenatide exposure are significantly increased in patients with severe renal impairment. Nevertheless, the increase in lixisenatide exposure (e.g., C_{max}) was not reported (Highlights of Clinical Pharmacology).*
L1028: ## 3.2.6.3 Instructions with Regard to Meals
L1029: Lixisenatide or lixisenatide placebo was administered as a subcutaneous periumbilical injection 15 minutes prior to breakfast at approximately 8:00 AM and 15 minutes prior to dinner at about 6:00 PM from Day 1 to Day 27, in addition to a morning dose on Day 28 and the Day -1 run-in.
L1030: *Reviewer's Comment: Acceptable. Since lixisenatide is administered as subcutaneous injection, food effects are not anticipated.*
L1031: ## 3.2.6.4 ECG and PK Assessments
L1032: "The assessment of ventricular repolarization was performed using 24-hour continuous 12-lead ECG recordings (Holter) on Day -1/Day 1 and Day 28/Day 29. Triplicate 10-second recordings were extracted from the Holter monitor at prespecified timepoints ranging from 30 minutes prior to investigational product administration to 24 hours afterward (T0.5H, T1H, T1.5H, T2H, T3H, T4H, T5H, T6H, T8H, T10H, T12H, and T24H).
L1033: "Plasma samples were collected at baseline (Day 1 predose); at T0.5H, T1H, T1.5H, T2H, T3H, T4H, T5H, T6H, T8H, T10H, T12H, and T24H on Day 28 postdose, and for assessment of Ctrough in the morning predose on Days 8, 15, 22, and 26 to 28."
L1034: *Reviewer's Comment: ECG/PK sampling schedule appears to be reasonable.*
L1035: ## 3.2.6.5 Baseline
L1036: Time-matched Day-1 ECG measures were used as baseline.
L1037: ## 3.2.7 ECG Collection
L1038: Intensive 12-Lead Holter monitoring was used to obtain digital ECGs. Standard 12-Lead ECGs was obtained while subjects are recumbent.
L1039: The assessment of ventricular repolarization was done using a 12-lead ECG (Marquette 5500) after at least 10 minutes in the supine position, before pharmacokinetic sampling.
L1040: Each ECG consisted of a 10 second recording of the 12 leads simultaneously, leading to:- a single 12-lead ECG (25 mm/s, 10 mm/mV) printout for immediate safety and quality evaluation (HR, PR, QRS, QT, QTc automatic correction) including date, time, initials, and number of subject, signature of the Research Physician, and at least 3 complexes for each lead;
L1041: - a duplicated digital storage, which enabled manual reading (centralized reading of computerized ECGs), where applicable: each digital file was to be identified by
L1042: theoretical time (day and time PxxDxxTxxHxx), real date and real time (recorder time), sanofi-aventis study code, and subject number (eg. 3 digits) and initials (eg. 3 digits).
L1043: A computer-assisted, manual, on-screen measurement of digital ECG waveforms was secondarily performed using a standardized methodology.
L1044: ECG of each subject was presented to the reader in random order with no sequence identification information.
L1045: ## 3.2.8 Sponsor's Results
L1046: ## 3.2.8.1 Study Subjects
L1047: All 91 subjects randomized to the study were evaluated for safety. Since the prematurely discontinued 4 subjects had neither ECG nor plasma concentration measurements on Day 28, they have been excluded from pharmacodynamic and pharmacokinetic analysis.
L1048: Three out of the 4 dropouts were replaced.
L1049: A greater percentage of male subjects (58.2%) were enrolled in the study compared to female subjects (41.8%).

L1079: ## 3.2.8.2.4 Additional Analyses
L1080: ## Heart rate analysis
L1081: The same statistical model was used to evaluate the \Delta\DeltaHR for treatment groups. The largest mean change of HR from baseline comparing to placebo is 7.29 bpm for 20-\mug
L1082: q.d. lixisenatide dose group, and 8.63 bpm for 30-\mug b.i.d. lixisenatide dose group both occurred at 4 hour. The 95% upper limits were 8.96 bpm and 10.30 bpm, respectively.
L1083: ## 3.2.8.3 Safety Analysis
L1084: Of the 91 subjects randomized, 4 subjects discontinued the study: 2 subjects discontinued the study on Day 1 due to technical problems with the ECG device, and 2 subjects discontinued due to an AE (due to urticaria and pruritic rash, withdrawal on Day 14 and Day 22, respectively).
L1085: The most frequent TEAEs were nausea and vomiting, which occurred mainly in the AVE0010 dose groups. There was only one subject (20 \mug q.d. AVE0010) with a single episode of vomiting on Day 28 about 2 hours after dosing and lasting about 1 minute. Most of these gastrointestinal events were mild to moderate in intensity. The 2 AEs, urticaria (30 \mug b.i.d. AVE0010) and pruritic rash (20 \mug q.d. AVE0010), resulted in premature withdrawal of the two affected subjects (on Day 14 and Day 22); both subjects developed anti-AVE0010 antibodies. In addition, few incidences of headache was also reported in both AVE0010 and placebo groups.
L1086: There were no SAEs reported in the study. There were no deaths reported in the study.

L1098: ## 3.2.8.4.2 Exposure-Response Analysis
L1099: The sponsor performed PK/PD descriptive analyses including overlays, histogram, hysteresis, and scatter plots. Overlay plots show lixisenatide concentrations and delta delta or delta parameter (QTcF, HR, QTcN, QT, or PR) versus time. Histograms show distributions of time of maximum or minimum delta delta or delta parameter (QTcF, HR, QTcN, QT, or PR) overlaid with time of largest lixisenatide concentration. Hysteresis plots show delta delta or delta parameter (QTcF, HR, QTcN, QT, or PR) and lixisenatide concentrations. Scatter plots show delta delta or delta parameter (QTcF, HR, QTcN, QT, or PR) versus lixisenatide concentration and log of concentration. Plots are provided for all subjects in the PK/PD population and separately by anti-lixisenatide antibody status.
L1100: Reviewer's Comments: We conducted our own independent analysis. A plot of \Delta\Delta QTcF vs. lixisenatide concentrations is presented in Figure 4.
L1101: ## 4 REVIEWERS' ASSESSMENT
L1102: ## 4.1 EVALUATION OF THE QT/RR CORRECTION METHOD
L1103: We evaluated the appropriateness of the correction methods (QTcF and QTcN (study-specific correction)) submitted by the sponsor. Baseline values were excluded in the validation. Ideally, a good correction QTc would result in no relationship of QTc and RR intervals.
L1104: We also evaluated the linear relationships between different correction methods (QTcF, QTcN) and RR. The mixed model shows a smaller slope of QTcF vs. RR than that of QTcN vs. RR, which indicates that QTcF is a better correction method than QTcI for this study. We also looked at the Mean Sum of Squared Slopes (MSSS). The smaller this value is, the better the correction. Again, QTcF produces the smallest MSSS. Therefore, the FDA reviewer used QTcF for the primary statistical analysis. Sponsor also used QTcF as primary outcomes. We evaluated both QTcF and QTcN methods and found out that the results based on both methods are similar.
L1105: Table 6: Comparison of QTcF and QTcN Using the Mixed Model
L1106: Treatment Groups	Slope of QTcF	Slope of QTcN	diff_p_value
All	0.01145	0.02780	<.001
Lixisenatide 20 ug q.d.	0.01013	0.02645	<.001
Lixisenatide 30 ug b.i.d.	0.01666	0.03330	<.001

L1156: ## 4.3 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY ASSESSMENTS
L1157: The mean lixisenatide concentration-time profile is illustrated in Figure 1. The overall exposure as well as the variability of the pharmacokinetic parameters increased largely in the antibody-positive subjects. The mean C_{max} and AUC of 30 µg b.i.d. dose was ~1.4-fold compared with lixisenatide 20 µg q.d. in the antibody-negative subjects or the antibody-positive subjects.
L1158: The relationship between ΔΔQTcF and lixisenatide concentrations is visualized in Figure 4. Based on visual diagnostics, the exposure-response relationship can be described better with a log-linear model compared to that with a linear model. As shown in Figure 4, the slope of ΔΔQTcF versus log lixisenatide concentration is positive and significant (slope 1.5266 with p-value &lt;.0001 for a log-linear mixed effect model including intercept). However, there is no substantial QT prolongation in the exposure range at the therapeutic dose of 20 µg q.d. and the supratherapeutic dose of 30 mg b.i.d. (Figure 4, bottom right panel). As stated earlier, it is not clear if 30 µg b.i.d. dose covers the high exposure clinical scenario (severe renal impairment).
L1159: Figure 4: \Delta\Delta QTcF vs. Lixisenatide Concentration
L1160: ## 4.4 CLINICAL ASSESSMENTS
L1161: ## 4.4.1 Safety assessments
L1162: None of the events identified to be of clinical importance per the ICH E 14 guidelines i.e. syncope, seizure, significant ventricular arrhythmias or sudden cardiac death occurred in this study.
L1163: ## 4.4.2 ECG assessments
L1164: Waveforms from the ECG warehouse were reviewed. Measurements were performed on the 'global' presentation of superimposed representative (median) PQRST complexes from all leads. According to ECG warehouse statistics less than 0.2% of ECGs reported to have significant QT bias, according to the automated algorithm. Overall ECG acquisition and interpretation in this study appears acceptable.
L1165: ## 4.4.3 PR and QRS Interval
L1166: Overall 17 subjects had PR &gt; 200 ms. However none of them had a PR &gt; 218 ms. Post-baseline increases did not exceed 20% from baseline values.
L1167: There were 16 subjects who experienced QRS interval greater than 110 ms in drug treatment groups. No subject had a post baseline QRS &gt; 118 ms or an increase over baseline &gt; 10%.
L1168: ## 5 APPENDIX
L1169: ## 5.1 HIGHLIGHTS OF CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
L1170: Therapeutic dose	20 µg QD1
Maximum tolerated dose	Single dose: 40 µg
Multiple dose: Above 30 µg BID after dose titration2
Principal adverse events	Nausea, vomiting, abdominal pain, diarrhoea, flatulence, headache, asthenia and hypoglycemia (mostly in patients receiving concomitant sulfonylureas, not observed in healthy subjects). In a few cases allergic events including one anaphylactic reaction.
Maximum dose tested	Single Dose	40 µg3
Multiple Dose	30 µg BID4
Exposures achieved at Maximum tested Dose [Mean (CV%)]	Single Dose	10 µg5:
- Cmax = 60.3 (35%) pg/mL
- AUC = 396 (31%) pg*h/mL
Multiple Dose	30 µg BID5:
- Cmax = 184 (36%) pg/mL
- AUClast = 866 (34%) pg*h/mL
Range of linear PK	Linear PK was shown between 5-20 µg QD or BID.
Accumulation at steady state	No accumulation in antibody negative subjects. In case of antibody formation, potential individual accumulation depending on properties of the multiclonal antibodies.
Metabolites	No known active metabolites. Peptide drugs are normally degraded into smaller peptides and eventually to amino acids and renally cleared.
Absorption	Absolute/Relative Bioavailability	No absolute BA study conducted. Rel. BA study testing site of SC injection with similar AUCs for abdomen, thigh and arm
Tmax	Median (range) for parent: 1.25 [0.75-2.25] h
Median (range) for metabolites: not applicable
Distribution	Vz/F [Mean (CV%)]	242 (58%) L (at 20 µg QD5)
% bound	The protein binding of lixisenatide was determined in vitro in human plasma by means of the ultracentrifugation method.
L1171: Unbound levels of lixisenatide were quantified using an ELISA assay. Lixisenatide showed a moderate (55%) binding to human plasma proteins in the 500 to 50,000 pg/mL concentration range.
Elimination	Route	The expected consequence of medium-sized peptides is the degradation to small peptides and individual amino acids.
Normally low molecular weight proteins as lixisenatide (~5000 Dalton) are filtered in the glomerulus, re-absorbed and proteolytically degraded in the renal tubulus.
Terminal t_{1/2}	Mean (%CV) for parent: 3.62 (65.1%) h.
Mean (%CV) for metabolites: not applicable.
CL/F or CL	Mean (% CV): 25.75 (37.5%) L/h.
Intrinsic Factors	Age	So far no indication of age effect on PK.
Sex	So far no indication of sex effect on PK.
Race	In PDY6797 20-30 % difference in exposure level between Japanese vs. Caucasian, probably accounted to the body weight difference in the two ethnicities.
Hepatic & Renal Impairment	No data available in hepatic impairment.
No relevant differences in C_{max} and AUC in patients with mild and moderate renal impairment. Significant increase in severe renal impairment.
Extrinsic Factors	Drug interactions	Lixisenatide has no inhibitory effect on drug metabolizing enzymes, interaction may occur due to delayed gastric emptying. For several compounds tested no effect on extent of absorption (AUC) if coadministered with lixisenatide was found.
Due to the route of application (s.c.) an effect of other compounds on lixisenatide PK is unlikely.
Food effects	Not applicable.
Expected High Clinical Exposure Scenario	30 µg lixisenatide BID, which is threefold the daily therapeutic dose of 20 µg QD, has been investigated in clinical studies DRI6012 (T2D patients), TES6865 and in TES11807 (thorough QTc studies with healthy subjects). 30µg BID is considered a supratherapeutic dose.
Higher doses have not been tested but cases of overdose were
L1172: observed in the 13-week dose-ranging study DR16012. Overdose did not result in specific adverse reactions, and sometimes were even asymptomatic.
The exposure ratio for C_{max} and AUC between 20 µg OD and 30 µg BID was 2.1 and 2.66.
Cases of relative overdose may occur by non-compliance with the titration scheme, i.e. instead of a starting dose of 10 µg liraglutide OD a patient injects 20 µg of liraglutide OD. In this case the patient may experience gastrointestinal events, mostly nausea and less frequently vomiting.
L1173: - route of injection was subcutaneous, refers to all doses mentioned in this table
- multiple dose &gt;30µg BID not tested
- in T2D patients, FIM study AVE0010A01-016
- in T2D patients, for 8 weeks after prior uptitration, Phase IIIb study DR10012
- single dose PK data from higher doses not available
- from TES6865 in antibody negative healthy subjects after 4-weeks treatment incl. 2 weeks maintenance dosing with 30µg BID

L1207: ## **Comment to Sponsor:**
L1208: In your Study POP6053 entitled "*Pharmacokinetics and safety of AVE0010 given as a single 5 µg subcutaneous dose in subjects with different degrees of renal impairment and in healthy subjects in an open, parallel group, non-randomized, single-dose study*", you have used creatinine clearance range cut-offs based on the old guidance. Please reanalyze the renal impairment data based on the cut-offs indicated in the current guidance (dated 03/22/10, located at http://www.fda.gov/downloads/Drugs/GuidanceComplianceRegulatoryInformation/Guidances/UCM204959.pdf).

L1230: ## Filing Memo
L1231: The sponsor had originally submitted a New Drug Application (NDA 204961) for lixisenatide. This NDA was reviewed from a Clinical Pharmacology perspective and was recommended for approval (documents located in DARRTS: (a) Clinical Pharmacology primary review, dated 08/27/2013, reference ID: 3362819, and (b) Clinical Pharmacology filing review, dated 02/12/2013, reference ID: 3259462). The sponsor withdrew the NDA prior to the action date, stating that it would be re-submitted at a later date along with results from an ongoing CVOT study. The sponsor re-submitted the NDA as NDA 208471. Since the original studies that were reviewed as part of NDA 204961 were re-submitted, those studies will not be reviewed again. The reader is referred to the Clinical Pharmacology review for NDA 204961 for information on the studies that were submitted previously. The Clinical Pharmacology review of the current NDA (208471) will include only studies that were not part of NDA 204961. Cross-reference to NDA 204961 will be made as needed throughout the Clinical Pharmacology review of NDA 208471.

7	POT12625	Investigate effects of repeated subcutaneous doses of lixisenatide 20 µg compared to liraglutide 1.2 mg or 1.8 mg in reducing postprandial plasma glucose after a standardized solid breakfast; on postprandial plasma glucose excursion; plasma glucose; C-peptide; glucagon; HbA1c; body weight and waist circumference; heart rate and blood pressures; appetite perceptions; safety and tolerability.

L1272: Figure 1 is a bar chart showing the Least Squares (LS) mean change in HbA1c (%) at endpoint for the Intention-to-Treat (ITT) population across different dosing regimens (QD: once daily, BID: twice daily) and doses. The Y-axis represents the LS mean change in HbA1c (%), ranging from -1.0 to 0.0. The X-axis shows the treatment groups: Placebo, 5 µg, 10 µg, 20 µg, and 30 µg for both QD and BID regimens. The number of patients (n) is indicated for each group.
Baseline overall mean HbA1c is 7.55%. The Placebo group (n=107) shows a mean change of -0.18%.
For the QD regimen, the mean changes are: 5 µg (-0.47%, P=0.0056), 10 µg (-0.50%, P=0.0033), 20 µg (-0.69%, P<0.0001), and 30 µg (-0.76%, P<0.0001).
For the BID regimen, the mean changes are: 5 µg (-0.65%, P<0.0001), 10 µg (-0.78%, P<0.0001), 20 µg (-0.75%, P<0.0001), and 30 µg (-0.87%, P<0.0001).
L1273: Figure 2 is a cumulative distribution function (CDF) plot showing the cumulative probability of change from baseline in HbA1c at week 13 for two dosing regimens: AVE0010 10 µg BID (red line) and AVE0010 20 µg QD (blue line). The X-axis represents the Change from Baseline in HbA1c, ranging from approximately -1.0 to 0.0. The Y-axis represents the Cumulative Probability, ranging from 0.00 to 1.00. Both curves show a dose-response relationship, with the 20 µg QD dose generally resulting in a greater mean change (lower HbA1c values) compared to the 10 µg BID dose.
L1274: Figure 3 displays two graphs showing the Mean (SD) Plasma Lixisenatide Concentration (pg/mL) versus Nominal Time (hr) after a single subcutaneous dose of 20 µg.
The left graph, titled "Normal Healthy Volunteers," shows a rapid increase in concentration peaking around 2 hours (approx. 130 pg/mL) and then declining. The concentration remains above the Limit of Quantification (LOQ = 12 pg/mL) for approximately 8 hours.
The right graph, titled "T2DM Patients," shows a similar profile, peaking around 2 hours (approx. 75 pg/mL) and declining. The concentration remains above the LOQ for approximately 8 hours.
Both graphs include error bars representing standard deviation (SD).
L1275: Figure 4 displays four graphs showing the Mean (SD) Plasma Lixisenatide Concentration (pg/ml) versus Nominal Time (hr) for multiple subcutaneous (SC) doses of lixisenatide in different volunteer groups.
Top Left: Normal Healthy Volunteers (Day 14 data)
Lixisenatide dose: 20 µg QD.
Data shows morning and evening administration. Concentration peaks around 2 hours (approx. 120-130 pg/ml) and declines by 8 hours (approx. 20 pg/ml).
Top Right: Normal Healthy Chinese Volunteers (Day 14 data)
Lixisenatide dose: 10 µg QD and 20 µg QD.
Data shows concentrations for 10 µg QD (open circles) and 20 µg QD (filled circles). Both doses peak around 2 hours (approx. 130-140 pg/ml) and decline by 8 hours (approx. 20 pg/ml). LOQ = 12 pg/ml.
Bottom Left: Overweight to Obese Volunteers (Day 182 data)
Lixisenatide dose: 20 µg QD.
Data shows concentrations for Day 182 ADA negative (open circles) and ADA positive (filled circles). ADA positive volunteers show significantly higher concentrations, peaking around 4 hours (approx. 650 pg/ml) and declining by 24 hours (approx. 400 pg/ml). ADA negative volunteers show low concentrations (below LOQ). LOQ = 12 pg/ml.
Bottom Right: Normal Healthy Volunteers (Day 28 data)
Lixisenatide dose: 20 µg QD and 30 µg BID.
Data shows concentrations for 30 µg BID (open circles), 20 µg QD (filled circles), and 20 µg QD Day 28 ADA negative (open squares). All groups peak around 4 hours (approx. 900-1000 pg/ml) and decline by 24 hours (approx. 200-300 pg/ml). LOQ = 12 pg/ml.
L1276: Figure 5: Mean plasma concentration time profile of lixisenatide after multiple SC doses of 10 µg and 20 µg. The graph plots Mean (SD) Plasma Lixisenatide Concentration (pg/ml) on the Y-axis (0 to 200) against Nominal Time (hr) on the X-axis (0 to 12). Two dose groups are shown: Lixisenatide 20µg QD (open circles) and Lixisenatide 10µg QD (filled circles). Both curves show a rapid increase in concentration, peaking around 1.5 to 2 hours, followed by a gradual decline. The 20µg QD group consistently shows approximately double the concentration of the 10µg QD group across the measured time points, indicating dose proportionality. Error bars represent standard deviation (SD). A dashed line indicates the Limit of Quantification (LOQ) at 12 pg/ml.
L1277: Figure 6: Line graph showing Mean & SE blood glucose values (mg/dL) over 16 hours (Planned time from morning injection) for the placebo group at baseline (Day -1) and at the highest well-tolerated dose. The placebo group shows a typical postprandial glucose profile, peaking around 250 mg/dL after breakfast and lunch, and around 200 mg/dL after dinner.
L1278: Figure 7 is a line graph showing Mean \pm SE blood glucose values (mg/dL) over 16 hours (Planned time from morning injection) for the lixisenatide QD group. L1281: Schematic study design for the Lixisenatide vs. Liraglutide study. The study duration is 35 ± 2 days. The timeline includes a Screening phase (Day -14 to -3), Inclusion phase (Day -1 to 1), 2-week Titration (Day 1 to 14), and 2-week maintenance dose (Day 14 to 28). The primary endpoint is assessed on Day 28. The study compares two treatment groups: Lixisenatide (n=60) starting at 10 µg and titrating up to 20 µg, and Liraglutide (n=60) starting at 0.6 mg and titrating up to 1.8 mg. All patients received a stable dose of Metformin throughout the study.

L1307: Diagram illustrating the pharmacokinetic model. A dose of Acetaminophen (AA_dose) transits from the stomach to the gut compartment (AA_gut) via k_trans(Lixi_p). AA_gut is absorbed into the blood stream (AA_p) via k_abs. AA_p is eliminated via k_exc. Lixisenatide (Lixi_dose) is absorbed into the blood stream (Lixi_p) via k_abs(L). Lixi_p is eliminated via k_exc(L).

L1335: Figure 10 displays four basic goodness-of-fit plots for the Sponsor's final model, arranged in a 2x2 grid.

Top Right Plot: Scatter plot of \ln(\text{observed concentration}) versus \ln(\text{individual predicted concentration}). The data points generally follow a dashed diagonal line, indicating a good fit for the individual model.
Bottom Left Plot: Scatter plot of Conditional weighted residuals versus Time after dose (h). The residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at 0, suggesting no systematic bias over time.
Bottom Right Plot: Scatter plot of Individual weighted residuals versus \ln(\text{individual predicted concentration}). The residuals are scattered randomly around the horizontal line at 0, suggesting no systematic bias related to the predicted concentration.

L1349: Study PKD12625
7-point SMPG profiles (mg/dL) at baseline and Day 56 – PD population
Corrected C-peptide (ng/mL) on Day-3 and Day 56 – PD population
C-Peptide: On Day 56, the corrected C-peptide concentrations were lower compared to Day -3 values up to 3 hours postbreakfast (T3H30 post lisdexamfetamine injection) but were slightly higher at 5 hours postbreakfast (T5H0 post lisdexamfetamine injection). In both the 1.2 mg and 1.8 mg groups, corrected C-peptide concentrations were higher compared to Day -3 (baseline) up to 5 hours postbreakfast (T5H30) with a peak at 2 hours postbreakfast (T2H30 post IMP injection) in the 1.2 mg group and at 3 hours postbreakfast (T3H0 post IMP injection) in the 1.8 mg group.

L1351: Study PKD12625
Corrected glucose (mg/mL) on Day-3 and Day 56 – PD population
Mean (\pm SEM) HbA1c (%) on Day 1 and Weeks 4 and 8
On Day 56, corrected glucose concentrations were lower compared to baseline (Day -3) in all treatment groups. The 1.2 mg and 1.8 mg groups showed a lower peak compared to the 1.2 and 1.8 mg groups on Day 56.
On Day 56, mean HbA1c levels were 7% (43 [10.5%] patients in the 1.2 mg group and 44 [100%] and 45 [97.8%] patients in the 1.8 mg group, respectively. In addition, most of the patients had HbA1c levels \le 6.5% (38 [82.6%] in the 1.2 mg group and 37 [84.1%] and 42 [91.3%] patients in the 1.8 mg group, respectively).
L1352: Results
EF113319
Mean change in HbA_{1c} (%) from baseline by scheduled visit - ITT population	Mean change in body weight (kg) from baseline by scheduled visit - ITT population

Patients in both treatment groups had a comparable mean HbA_{1c} at baseline (7.72% for liraglutide and 7.64% for placebo). Mean HbA_{1c} was reduced from baseline in both treatment groups, but with a greater reduction in the liraglutide group at each observation over the entire study; this reduction was most pronounced over the first 12 weeks of treatment.
In the liraglutide group, body weight decreased from baseline over the first 12 weeks of the study (mean change: -0.61 kg) and then returned to approximately baseline levels. At Week 108 (1.2 years) the mean change from baseline was 0.37 kg. In the placebo group, mean body weight remained constant over the first 12 weeks of the study and then progressively increased. At Week 108, the mean change from baseline was 1.11 kg.
Line graph showing Mean change in HbA1c (%) from baseline by scheduled visit - ITT population. The combination group shows a greater reduction in HbA1c compared to the placebo group.
L1353: Results
ACT12374
Mean 7-point self monitored plasma glucose (SMPG) profiles (mmol/L) at baseline and Week 24 (LOCF) - mITT population	Mean change in body weight (kg) from baseline by visit - mITT population

The combination-treated patients had a statistically significant greater reduction in mean change in average 7-point SMPG profile from baseline to Week 24 compared to the insulin glargine-treated patients (LS mean difference of -0.30 mmol/L [5.48 mg/dL]; p-value = 0.0154).
A statistically significant difference was observed between treatment groups for the mean change in body weight from baseline to Week 24; LS mean body weight decreased by 0.97 kg for the combination-treated patients and increased by 0.48 kg for the insulin glargine-treated patients; LS mean difference versus insulin glargine = -1.44 kg; p-value <0.0001.
Line graph showing Mean 7-point self monitored plasma glucose (SMPG) profiles (mmol/L) at baseline and Week 24 (LOCF) - mITT population. The combination group shows a greater reduction in SMPG compared to the placebo group.
L1354: Results
ACT12374
Mean HbA_{1c} (%) by visit - mITT population	Mean plasma glucose (mmol/L) during meal test at baseline and Week 24 (LOCF) - mITT population

The least squared (LS) mean changes from baseline to Week 24 in HbA_{1c} were -1.42% for the combination group and -1.44% for the insulin glargine group reaching a mean HbA_{1c} level of 6.2% and 6.5% at Week 24 respectively. LS mean difference between the combination group and insulin glargine group was -0.17% with 95% CI of -0.322% to -0.037%. Based on the pre-specified primary analysis, the non-inferiority of the combination group compared to the insulin glargine group was demonstrated, as the upper bound of the 2-sided 95% CI of the LS mean difference was less than the predefined non-inferiority margin of 0.4%. Statistical superiority of the combination over insulin glargine was also demonstrated (LS mean difference versus insulin glargine = -0.17%; p-value = 0.0130).
A statistically significant greater reduction in 2-hour PPG from baseline to Week 24 after a standardized meal was observed in the combination group compared to the insulin glargine group; LS mean change from baseline to Week 24 was -7.49 mmol/L [-134.99 mg/dL] for the combination group and -4.33 mmol/L [-77.92 mg/dL] for the insulin glargine group; LS mean difference = -3.17 mmol/L [-57.07 mg/dL]; p-value <0.0001.
Line graph showing Mean HbA1c (%) by visit - mITT population. The combination group shows a greater reduction in HbA1c compared to the placebo group.
